MARKET VIEW
The Bull Market Turns Four: Older, Unloved—But Still Kicking
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Four years ago this month, virtually no one was in the mood to buy U.S. Equities, and for
good reasons.
Rewind to October 2022: Inflation the month before clocked in at an annual rate of 8.2%.

The yield curve was inverted, a signal that has preceded most past recessions. Consumer
sentiment was abysmal, having dropped to a record low in June 2022. Recession
concerns were mounting, with an October Wall Street Journal poll survey putting the
odds of a recession within 12 months at about 63%. And realizing (belatedly) that
inflation was not “transitory,” the Fed was in the midst of administering one of the most
aggressive monetary tightening cycles in decades, raising the fed funds rate five times

between March and September 2022 by a stunning 300 basis points.
Reflecting the mood at the moment, the S&P 500 closed at 3,577 on October 12, 2022,
down 25% below its January peak, leaving investors battered and bruised but blind to the
fact that the market had bottomed. Four years on, the bull market that emerged from
the gloom of 2022 is still standing and kicking, with the S&P 500 up roughly 130% since
the October 2022 lows.
The more than doubling of the S&P over the past four years has hardly been linear—and

has been accompanied with plenty of pain and doubt. Exhibit 2 maps the rocky path
trodden by the bull over the past four years. Less than six months into the rally, the
markets were rocked by the failure of Silicon Valley Bank (SVB) on March 10, 2023. The
S&P Regional Banks Select Industry Index lost 18% that week and stoked concerns of
massive deposit runs and credit stress across the capital markets. However, owing to
emergency intervention by the government, lower yields and strength elsewhere in the

markets, the crisis passed. Some banks failed that month, but the bull market didn’t.
Exhibit 2: Revisiting the Rocky Path Trodden by the Bull.
Period "Reason to Sell" What happened instead
October 2022 Inflation >8%, aggressive Fed tightening, recession concerns Bull market begins
March 2023 SVB/regional banking crisis Stocks keep climbing
2023-24 Inverted yield curve, “recession inevitable” Economy keeps expanding
2024 “Magnificent 7* bubble,” narrow breadth, expensive valuations Earnings propel market higher

2025 Tariffs, trade war, policy uncertainty Market absorbs another shock
2026 Oil/diesel shock, 5%+ yields, Fed tightening, geopolitical risks Bull reaches fourth birthday
*Apple, Amazon, Alphabet, Nvidia, Meta, Microsoft and Tesla. Source: Chief Investment Office. As of October 9, 2026.
But recessionary concerns hung like threatening clouds over the markets for most of
2023. Aggressive Fed tightening, an inverted yield curve, and soft economic data were

all taken as harbingers of an economic slowdown and profits recession. Yet the S&P 500
continued to climb in 2023, up 26.3% for the year, driven in large part by the outsized
price performance of the Magnificent Seven.
Investors spent much of 2024 fretting about a market too dependent on technology,
and therefore a market too narrow and too expensive. The unwinding of the Japan carry
trade in August 2024 engendered more angst, as did uncertainties around the 2024

U.S. presidential election. But despite a host of misgivings, the S&P 500 delivered total
returns of 25% in 2024. The unloved bull marched on.
Total returns in 2025 were robust as well—roughly 18%—notwithstanding—yet
again—a number of headwinds. In late January, DeepSeek, a cheaper China AI model,
raised questions about America’s technology leadership and the need to spend enormous
sums on chips and data centers. The Nasdaq promptly dropped by 3.1% thereafter.

Investment Implications
The adoption phase of the AI buildout
should translate to broader earnings
growth and performance across
sectors, reinforcing our conviction
in U.S. Equities. Given a strong
fundamental backdrop, we would
continue to buy on weakness as the
bull enters its fifth year

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
January’s jolt was followed by “Liberation Day,” or the warning of punishing U.S. tariffs
on America’s major trading partners. The trade-policy shock of April 2025 caused the
S&P 500 to drop nearly 19% from its February high to its April 8 low on uncertainty
that higher import costs would decrease corporate margins and consumer spending and
upend global supply chains. Amid the trade tumult, Treasury prices fell and the U.S. dollar

weakened on concerns that foreigners were abandoning U.S. assets. Remember all the
angst over the dollar debasement trade? If that wasn’t enough, expensive valuations and
enormous capital commitments raised doubts about the AI trade and triggered talk of an
AI bubble. Yet despite this wall of worry, the S&P 500, for the third year running, posted
double-digit gains in 2025.
And double-digit gains could be in store this year, with the S&P 500 up 14% through

October 9. The bull has rumbled on, taking in stride the energy shock from the war in
the Middle East, the shift in interest rate expectations from the Fed (from rate cuts
to rate hikes), and the rising uncertainty associated with the midterm elections. These
headwinds have been countered by tailwinds such as better-than-expected corporate
earnings and better-than-anticipated real growth.
Finally, as Exhibit 3 makes clear, what has given this bull so much muscle has been

the U.S. Technology sector, specifically the Magnificent 7. Powering the bull has been
the Magnificent 7, with a staggering 344% return since the 2022 lows, while U.S.
technology has delivered a 277% return versus the S&P 500’s return of 130%. Note the
outperformance of the S&P 500 relative to the main international indices.
Looking forward: Is the bull’s greatest strength also its Achilles’ heel? Bull
markets don’t die of natural causes. And they typically don’t expire because of one

particular signal or indicator. Bull markets turn to bear markets when a cluster of signals/
metrics turn south. Think deteriorating and downward earnings revisions, higher interest
rates, widening credit spreads and tighter lending standards, growth disappointments,
stretched valuations and sentiment, deteriorating market breadth, and an external
shock. Any one of these signals could flash red and not trouble the bull.  But when they
converge or feed off each other, that’s trouble.

In addition, since a great deal of the earnings growth, market leadership and capital
expenditures (capex) spending of this bull market have come from the technology sector,
the bull’s greatest strength could become its greatest vulnerability. AI has become an
investment ecosystem unto itself, and hence the fifth year of the bull market will pivot
in part around AI revenue, frontier model adoption rates, infrastructure spending, free
cash flows, depreciation costs, debt issuance, data center utilization, cloud growth, and

competition via cheaper models and computing alternatives. To put it mildly, there is a
great deal at stake when it comes to the bull and AI.
However, this is where we stand today: We believe the bull will continue to rumble
ahead next year, with AI revenues and productivity gains justifying the costs of the AI
infrastructure. Critically, AI is moving from the buildout phase, dominated by chipmakers
and hyperscaler capex, into the adoption phase, where software firms, healthcare,

finance, energy and a myriad of other firms start to see productivity gains and margin
expansion from rising AI adoption rates. We believe this will give better breadth to the
S&P 500; so too will continued profit growth across various U.S. sectors, AI investment
that starts to pay off, and an economy capable of absorbing setbacks or periodic bouts
of uncertainty.
The path forward won’t be linear—and there will be fits and starts, and market
drawdowns. That’s normal for any market. But that aside, the bull will keep rumbling,

roaming and kicking in 2027.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is our view of the macro and market landscape heading
into 2027 and beyond?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
A supply side-led economic expansion continues, driven by capital investment in the AI
buildout, solid consumer spending and fiscal tailwinds still blowing, albeit with less force.
This creates a backdrop that keeps nominal growth well-above trend and, importantly,

above the interest cost on national debt.
The interest rate backdrop largely remains the same as of today, but, instead of the
sharp increases that we experienced in 2026, the curve should settle down, and yields
should remain relatively range bound. The Fed is likely to tap on the breaks a couple of
more times before reassessing new inflation, growth and employment trends later in
2027.
Concerns surrounding credit risk and "circular" financing are likely to rise the further we

move into the AI buildout, but we do not expect a default cycle to develop. Rather, we
expect the sharp backup in yields to place pressure namely on lower-quality credits in
areas having a difficult time building the cash flow necessary to pay the higher interest
costs.
We expect a broadening out within the market but technology to still maintain its
heavyweight positioning. As we move from training and evolving the large language
models to agentic, and physical AI new leaders are likely to develop. In addition, we

anticipate AI adopters such as biotechnology, transport, logistics, advance manufacturing
and financial companies to enter new growth and productivity phases.
Lastly, overall earnings growth in the U.S. should be low to mid double-digit percent
stages in 2027, which is about half of the growth from 2026 thus far but still around
double from normal levels.
Credit risks developing and higher energy prices for longer are the main risks to our bull
case for Equities extending further into 2027 and beyond.

Index Definitions
ecurities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

University of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index is a monthly survey of consumer confidence levels in the United States conducted by the University of Michigan.
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence measures what consumers are feeling about their expected financial situation, whether that's optimistic or pessimistic.
Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and

services.
Nasdaq Composite Index is a stock market measure that tracks the performance of more than 3,000 stocks listed on the Nasdaq exchange.
MSCI Emerging Markets c is a stock market index that tracks the performance of large-cap and mid-cap companies across 24 developing and fast-growing nations.
MSCI All-Country World Index ex USA is a market-capitalization-weighted stock market index that tracks large- and mid-cap equity performance across developed and emerging markets

worldwide, excluding the United States.
MSCI Europe, Australasia, and the Far East (EAFE) Index is a stock market index that measures the performance of large- and mid-cap companies across 21 developed.
Dow Jones Industrial Average Index is a stock market index that tracks 30 major public companies listed on the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) and Nasdaq.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is an alternative version of the standard S&P 500 that gives every constituent company an identical weight of 0.2% during each quarterly rebalance, rather

than weighting companies by their market capitalization.
Russell 2000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of approximately 2,000 small-cap (small-capitalization) public companies in the United States.
S&P 500 sub-sectors and industry groups Global Industry Classification Standard (GICS®)/S&P 500 Total Return Index, including Information Technology Total Return (TR) USD;
Consumer Discretionary TR USD; Industrials TR USD; Real Estate TR USD; Communication Services TR USD; Materials  TR USD; Financials TR USD; Consumer Staples TR USD; Utilities TR USD;

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

Dealmaker-in-Chief or Mr Veto?
The Republican majorities in the House and Senate in the ﬁrst half
of Trump's presidential term allowed him to sign the One Big
Beautiful Bill Act into law in July 2025. Other legislation included
the Laken Riley Act, which tightened immigration and border policy,
the GENIUS Act, which created a regulatory structure for

stablecoins, the HALT Fentanyl Act on drugs and the TAKE IT DOWN
Act on online regulation.

### Page 3

The obvious outcome from the result on Tuesday is that a
Democrat-controlled Congress will stiﬂe President Trump’s domestic
agenda in his ﬁnal two years in power. A bill has to be approved by
both the House and the Senate before being passed on to the
president to sign or veto. With the Democrats having control of the
legislative agenda in the House and Senate, the president has
already warned that he will likely become “Mr Veto”. The only way
to overcome that would be for the Democrats to secure a two-thirds

supermajority in both houses. That isn't the case after last night's
results.
The consequence is that spending and tax proposals, as with most
domestic legislation, will likely be gridlocked. There are potential
areas of compromise tied to healthcare aﬀordability in exchange
for perhaps more spending on defence, but major tax and spending
changes appear unlikely.
On the one hand, this is often viewed positively, as it provides a
stable backdrop for corporate America. On the other, it means there

is little prospect of meaningful change to the nation’s ﬁscal stance.
While we won’t see any further ﬁscal expansion, large ﬁscal deﬁcits
and deteriorating debt dynamics will continue.
The ﬁscal situation will certainly remain a concern, although hitting
the debt ceiling could act as a temporary brake. The issue is likely
to come to a head by mid-summer 2027, with little scope for
compromise between the two sides in Congress. This risks an
extended government shutdown, potentially exceeding last

October’s shutdown, which depressed GDP growth in 4Q 2025 by a
full percentage point. The Federal Reserve risks “ﬂying blind” again
with no government economic statistics being collated or released.
What the Democrats can seek to achieve
Key sectors of corporate America will be nervous that Democratic
control of important oversight committees could bring greater
scrutiny. More congressional hearings involving AI and technology
business leaders would be a likely outcome, while a strong

performance at the ballot box may embolden local Democratic

leaders to try to stall unpopular data centre construction in their
constituencies. Fossil fuel producers may face similar challenges.
Democrats will likely feel even more conﬁdent about pushing back
against President Trump’s policies on Iran, support of Israel and any
escalation of tariﬀs. They may apply more pressure on the
president to sound tougher towards Russia and to provide support
to Ukraine, but the ﬁnal call will remain with the president.

Investigations into President Trump, his family and members of his
administration may also be stepped up as Democrats exercise their
greater powers. Inquiries into ﬁnances and business dealings could
result in subpoenas for documents and testimony. Moreover,
impeachment proceedings begin in the House, raising the
possibility of a third attempt to impeach the president.
To be convicted and removed from oﬃce, 67 of the 100 senators
would need to vote in favour, which is unlikely. The Democrats could

still use the process to constrain the president, but doing so risks
further toxifying the political situation in the US. Rather than
pursuing such action against the president himself, Democrats
could instead launch investigations into members of his cabinet.
With Democrats now in control of the Senate, they can block or, at
the very least, stall judicial and executive nominees. This includes
heads of government agencies, ambassadors and cabinet

members, potentially making it diﬃcult for the president to replace
any senior oﬃcials he chooses to ﬁre.
What will the president do?
A key question is whether a constrained President Trump, entering
the ﬁnal phase of his tenure, becomes more willing to accept
political or market disruption in pursuit of his objectives.
If there is little sense of any compromise, the ensuing gridlock in
Congress would leave the president to focus on areas where he can

exercise executive power: 1) foreign policy and diplomacy, including
trade policy, 2) national security and defence, 3) law enforcement
and regulatory policy, 4) personnel appointments (subject to
Senate approval), and 5) administration and management policy.

This suggests Iran, Russia/Ukraine and tariﬀs/trade policy are likely
to be the focus for the president. Tariﬀ policy could increasingly be
used to incentivise foreign partners to align with US foreign policy
objectives, for example by pressuring NATO members to increase
defence spending.
More fundamentally, heavy defeats in the House and Senate will be
interpreted as a referendum on President Trump’s policies and
potentially prompt ﬁgures within the Republican Party to take the

view that it is time to open the debate on its future direction. With
Trump’s inﬂuence set to fade, leading lights within the party, most
notably JD Vance and Marco Rubio, may attempt to shore up
support for their own campaigns as they look to the 2028
presidential election. Similarly, a large Democratic victory will
intensify speculation over whether the party’s 2028 candidate
should come from its more moderate wing or whether the
progressive left has enough momentum to put forward a candidate

of its own.
The market reaction
The initial bond market reaction has been a fall in yields across the
curve. In the coming days, that could reverse as the prospect of
policy stasis leaves the market weighing a still-elevated ﬁscal
deﬁcit against some easing of inﬂation concerns. We will probably
see front-end yields drift lower, while the back end retains an upside
bias. If we do see more tariﬀs, the back end could ﬁnd a small silver
lining in the additional tax revenues. But any beneﬁt is likely to be

marginal, given the risk that tariﬀs also push inﬂation higher.
Overall, we retain a bias towards the 10-year yield heading towards
5.25%.
The dollar is softer on the outcome, though it has recovered from its
lows seen in early Asia when a sharp fall in Nasdaq futures was
warning of a larger re-pricing of the tech story. Nasdaq futures have
since retraced the majority of those losses. Limiting a larger dollar
correction on this news is probably a still-resilient US economy and

at least one more rate hike from the Federal Reserve. The dollar
could see a little more weakness into 2027, however, if an

emboldened Democratic Party takes greater issue with Republican

What it means for Europe and China
With the outcome of the vote limiting President Trump’s options at
home, he is likely to focus more heavily on foreign and trade policy.
The result could be greater volatility and uncertainty for the global

economy.
This will clearly aﬀect Europe. A forced deal between Russia and
Ukraine, to the disadvantage of Ukraine and Europe, could
undermine economic sentiment in Europe. A new escalation in the
Middle East would push up energy prices and harm the European
economy. Europe’s latest shift towards more protectionist trade
policies could bring new global trade tensions.
At the same time, with only two years left in oﬃce, the TACO
(Trump Always Chickens Out) phenomenon could increasingly

shape Europe’s thinking. The risk of an unwarranted economic
accident rises as Trump’s policies towards Europe become more
aggressive and Europe’s own stance hardens, in the hope that
Trump will ultimately back down or simply run out of time.
If Republicans lay the blame for the poor result at the president’s
door, the sense that Trump’s time is coming to an end could grow
stronger. If so, some European countries may be tempted to test
the president’s patience and slow the pace of defence spending

increases, particularly given their own ﬁscal constraints.
Alternatively, and less likely, a stiﬂed but less angry Trump
administration could try to bring the wars in Iran and Ukraine to an
end. Constrained by Congress and the Supreme Court on trade, it
could ultimately, intentionally or otherwise, end up indirectly
supporting the European economy.
China’s main response will be to wait and see, as it will react to
actual policy changes rather than the shifts within Congress. The

main potential trigger points would be Taiwan arms sales, tariﬀs
and other trade/investment restrictions.

Personal ties between the two leaders appear good, but with
President Trump’s policy focus being channelled towards foreign
and trade policy, China could end up in the crosshairs again. The
trade truce is set to expire in January, and it seems like both sides
are not interested in going back to the mutually destructive peak
tariﬀ scenario we had in 2025, but it cannot be ruled out. As with
Europe, if Trump appears weakened by the outcome of the
midterms, China could sense that it is time to start looking beyond

2028, triggering a revaluation of its long-term US strategy.
Share

Document 394: Open mouth operations (2026-11-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Open mouth operations (2026-11-12).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #rba
Open mouth operations
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
13:34 October 23 2026 Share
The RBA does not want to promise what it will do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks.
Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for every nuance. Lately, there seems to have
been more RBA communication to pore over. The method of delivery has also evolved. This has

### Page 2

Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks for clues to future rate moves. They will
have plenty to pore over in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry of fireside chats
this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday.
Next Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before Senate Estimates, just as the
quarterly CPI is released. (This timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After the

decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a media release and press conference. More
speeches will follow, including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just following the RBA Review but also more
recently. There may be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication outside that certainly
feels more frequent than a couple of years ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little

sparse outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary appearances. We have counted
58 public engagements so far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website. This compares
with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in 2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events were related to economics, markets
and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed as well. There are fewer set-piece

speeches on policy and a lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few years ago, there
are more interviews across a wide range of media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It
was a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the 7:30 Report; it is hard to imagine him
agreeing to an Assistant Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general audience. Central banks want to explain

why they do what they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising but the household
sector is not the source of the inflation.
Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief
Communication Officer highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting media conference is
designed to speak to the public. Market participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
sometimes caused confusion when tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different tone and

varying complexity.
The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly using speeches directed to professional
audiences to discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such as neutral rates and
financial conditions. They are also highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more ways
than just squeezing mortgage holders.
One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises

about the future path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running commentary about how it
is interpreting recent data. These interpretations are themselves useful signals about what the RBA
thinks it might do.

### Page 3

the primary audience. So if you think that what the Governor said in the media conference sounded
more ‘dovish’ than what another official says in a more market-oriented forum, it is partly because she
is trying to convey that the RBA understands that its recent policy actions are making things harder for
many Australians. But this is in much the same way that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It
does, but that will not stop the treatment.

A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted material such as the minutes and set-
piece speeches than in unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be careful with their
words in unscripted settings. If the tone differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have reduced the pressure to make the set-piece

speeches more accessible. Research on central bank communication often uses measures such as
the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was
beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison suggests four speeches from 2026 (1, 2, 3,
4) were harder to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches scored in the 11 to 12
range, which is high-school-level complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and 15, which is

academic or technically dense text requiring university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-Governor Phil Lowe also scored better
on these metrics than the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that readability metrics do
not necessarily imply better clarity if the concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has

begun to put more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those invisible ‘star’ variables
(NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences
has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on the full range of transmission channels from
interest rates to inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour supply channel). This is

understandable in a world of supply shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt they would be comforted, though, by the
Deputy Governor’s explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the smallest of the
transmission channels from interest rates to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on inflation. It raises the question of whether

other policy tools or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on inflation through the other
channels without hitting a minority of households disproportionately.
We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases to summarise the position. From ‘narrow
path’ to ‘not ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once entrenched, a phrase can
outlast its usefulness.

### Page 4

Related articles
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record the presentations to the Board and the
members’ discussion. The minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-meeting
statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The
August 2024 minutes were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how sensitive the forecasts
were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed

Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s communication have not changed. It
wants to explain – and perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how it interprets
incoming data, especially inflation, the labour market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business of providing ‘forward guidance’ about
future moves. Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular future path for interest

rates. Some academic literature argues that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high, for
that matter) independently amplifies the transmission of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-
period interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield curve. Central banks including the RBA
have certainly tried jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view, though, is that the
potential cost of reputational loss if things do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a

slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides the signal about future policy directions. This
is why it was noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour market as ‘a little tight’ after the
September meeting, even though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We have been
noting for some time that the RBA staff have a downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the

RBA’s oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not
surprise us.

uncertainty persists

$90/bbl yesterday. While expectations of improving Middle Eastern
supply availability and bearish US inventory data have weighed on
the market, uncertainty around the US-Iran conﬂict continues to
provide some support.

expectations of a draw of around 0.6m barrels. Inventories at

Ewa Manthey and Warren Patterson
Gold extended its decline for a third session
Energy – Oil slips as Middle East output
rebounds
Oil prices edged lower this morning, with Brent around $91/bbl as
recovering Middle East supply eased immediate concerns over
availability. OPEC crude production increased by almost 2m b/d in
October to 27.16m b/d, led by Saudi Arabia, Iraq and Kuwait. Saudi
production climbed to 9.2m b/d, close to pre-war levels, while Iraq
produced almost 4.5m b/d.

Metals – Copper drops to trade near four-
month low
Copper traded near a four-month low this morning as renewed
selling in technology stocks and a ﬁrmer US dollar weighed on the
metals complex. LME prices brieﬂy fell to $13,657/t. The market has
pulled back from September’s record near $15,000/t, with investors

over US import tariﬀs remains unresolved. Further dollar strength
could keep copper under pressure in the near term.
Aluminium was also slightly weaker, although nearby LME spreads
remain exceptionally tight. Cash metal traded at a premium of
more than $150/t to the three-month contract this week, pointing
to continued short-term supply constraints.
Elsewhere, iron ore prices continued to retreat, with the Singapore

decline into a fourth day. Chinese iron ore futures also weakened,
alongside losses in rebar, as softer steel markets kept pressure on
the raw material complex. The move leaves iron ore trading close to
the psychologically important $90/t level. We remain bearish on
iron ore as weaker Chinese steel demand and growing seaborne
supply weigh on the outlook.
In precious metals, gold extended its decline for a third session,

slipping below $4,000/oz as stronger US inﬂation data and hawkish
comments from Federal Reserve oﬃcials pushed Treasury yields
higher. The market is increasingly pricing in the possibility of
another rate hike, providing support to the dollar and weighing on
non-yielding gold. Attention now turns to upcoming US inﬂation
data and further comments from Fed oﬃcials. While higher rates
and a stronger dollar could continue to weigh on gold in the near

term, we remain constructive over the longer term, supported by
central bank buying and safe-haven demand.

Document 416: The FOMC pause to reflect (2026-11-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/The FOMC pause to reflect (2026-11-12).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#fomc #usd #us
The FOMC pause to reflect
By Elliot Clarke
Head of International Economics, Westpac
08:14 October 29 2026 Share
No change in October, though confidence in the economy's strength and two dissents point
to a hike in December.
The FOMC held at their October meeting, albeit with two dissents, Governor Cook and Cleveland Fed
President Hammack preferring to hike by 25bps at this meeting. The statement was short and, in the
press conference, Chair Warsh again declined to provide definitive guidance on the near-term policy

### Page 2

Related articles
outlook.
The Committee remains positive on economic activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding
at a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in September). Despite elevated uncertainty, “domestic
spending has been resilient”, underpinned by “strong” productivity growth and “robust” capital
investment. Chair Warsh subsequently also emphasised the breadth of economic growth outside of
tech-related investment. Labour demand and supply were represented as balanced and resilient in

both the statement and press conference.
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in the statement. However, inflation “remains
elevated” and the FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable return of annual
consumer inflation to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target.
Along with the two dissents, this guidance and the “accumulation of more information will help the
Committee determine the policy stance” suggests another 25bp hike is likely in December. Thereafter,

the FOMC will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related risks through 2027.
Towards the end of the press conference, Chair Warsh also announced a structural change in the
FOMC schedule. While there will still be 8 meetings in 2027, a meeting in the fall will focus on
monetary policy strategy versus the current stance of monetary policy. Chair Warsh then intends for 2
of the 8 meetings in 2028 to have this strategy focus. If realised, this will see the FOMC review and

vote on the current stance of monetary policy just 6 times per calendar year from 2028.

Document 453: What’s the Renminbi Got to Do With it_ (2026-10-11)

Source PDF: all report pdf/What’s the Renminbi Got to Do With it_ (2026-10-11).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-10-11
Pages: 17
Status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
What’s the Renminbi Got to Do With it?
A closer look at the impact of China’s currency on China’s export growth.
Sean Yong/Reuters
UPDATED
October 11, 2026 12:00 a.m.
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
There is an argument—made in the recent Geneva
Report and by various former IMF officials—that
China’s current export surge is the simple
consequence of the housing downturn and the
resulting weakness in Chinese internal demand.*
The weakness in the RMB in this story is entirely
incidental to the increase in exports, which is, more or
less, a mechanical response to weak internal demand.

Cannot sell excavators at home? Export them.
Built more modern car capacity than your internal
market supports? Export that too…
The fall in the nominal RMB over the course of 2022
and the first half of 2023 in nominal and read terms
was simply a function of domestic macroeconomic
weakness, and a growing rate differential. It wasn’t an
active policy choice by China’s central bank.

### Page 3

3
Now, some might note that China’s currency doesn’t
always move with rate differentials. The RMB didn’t
consistently get pulled up when Chinese rates were
higher than U.S. rates (2002-2020 generally
speaking), and while it did fall when rate differentials
moved against the RMB in 2022 and 2023, it then
stabilized and has even appreciated modestly since
the middle of last year. That appreciation hasn’t been
big enough to correct the deep undervaluation of the

RMB, but it came at a time when the PBOC policy
rate (the reverse repo rate) was well below the U.S.
policy rate.
China doesn’t have an open financial account. As a
result, equilibrium in the foreign exchange market is
thus driven far more by exporter conversion and
current account flows than by rate differentials and
financial flows.** That, together with China’s ongoing
management of the exchange rate and a trade surplus
that can generate significant inflows, means that the

### Page 4

4
value of the RMB is much more of a policy variable
under the independent control of the Chinese
authorities than a simple byproduct of monetary and
fiscal policies.
The modern IMF is reluctant to accept this
conclusion. Its surveillance has generally ignored
China’s currency management (even if that
management is relatively obvious; see the data on FX
settlement) and its recent comprehensive assessment
of China’s economy completely ignored the balance of

payments and exchange rate management.
But it is pretty obvious to the market, even if the
PBOC, rather absurdly, insists that “market forces
play a decisive role in determining the RMB
exchange rate.”
I doubt the PBOC can win this argument. I agree
with Mark Sobel:
“The renminbi is still heavily managed and the
authorities have acted to slow appreciation. The paper
states that the PBoC phased out ‘regular’ foreign
exchange intervention in 2017. But it has many

means at its disposal to achieve the same effect
through extensive capital controls, administrative
guidance, signalling at the fix and deploying state-
owned commercial banks. These means are heavily
used. Thus, the word ‘regular’ must be taken with a
pinch of salt. The comparison to Japan, which

### Page 5

5
intervenes occasionally, is spurious – the yen largely
floats freely in integrated global markets. The same
cannot be said for the renminbi.”
In reality, the PBOC’s daily fix now plays the decisive
role in the currency’s path, and the market is often a
bet on how the PBOC’s management of the yuan will
evolve.
That is clear from the lack of volatility in the yuan’s
rate against the dollar. The yuan’s move down against
the dollar in 2022 was a controlled 10 percent move,

and the appreciation over the last 16 months has been
even more controlled crawl.
And the market is clearly massaged with substantial
help from the state banks. In fact that the pattern of
changes in the monthly FX settlement data looks
exactly like the pattern of intervention of a central
bank managing its currency around a tight band (the

### Page 6

6
state banks in practice buy FX to keep the CNY from
testing the strong side of the band, so there is an
asymmetry in how the CNY is managed)
None of this would matter much if China’s economy
was small. But that obviously isn’t the case. Nor would
this matter much if China’s exports didn’t respond to
changes in the RMB’s real (and nominal) value. But
that is also not the case.
There is abundant evidence that China’s exports do
respond to moves in the currency. Periods of RMB

weakness have consistently been associated with
periods of especially rapid Chinese export growth.

### Page 7

7
Chancellor Merz and President Macron got this
right, even if macroeconomic issues haven’t yet
figured prominently in the EU’s dialogue with
China.*** As they note in their open letter to the
European Commission:
“An appreciation of undervalued currencies towards a value
more in line with fundamentals would help support the
rebalancing of trade accounts with the EU in the short and
medium term … European policy discussions focused on
forging a common understanding of these exchange rate issues

and their interaction with global imbalances would be useful.”
The best evidence that exports responded to changes
in the currency, and not simply to changes in the path
of China’s internal demand, is the acceleration in
China’s export growth after the RMB’s depreciation
over the course of 2022.
This is the killer chart.

### Page 8

8
Now, you might ask: what of the acceleration in
export growth in 2020 and 2021? That was pretty
clearly tied to the global increase in goods demand
that came with the pandemic and work from home
requirements. It was expected to fade when the world
economy normalized. But it didn’t. By 2023 Chinese
export growth again topped global trade growth.
The general expectation that the rise in China’s goods
surplus during Covid would prove to be “transitory”

### Page 9

9
heavily influenced the IMF’s work at the time: see the
IMF’s external sector reports of 2023 and 2024.
I would argue that neglecting the exchange rate was
the key reason the IMF’s kept forecasting, incorrectly,
that China’s current account surplus would fall (See
this blog with Shahin Vallee). The IMF certainly
didn’t warn that China’s property downturn and the
RMB’s real depreciation risked a second China shock
in its 2022, 2023, or 2024 surveillance reports.

Concretely, the weaker RMB appears to have
contributed to a very large outperformance of China’s
exports relative to global trade. See the following
chart
That incidentally also happened after the RMB
followed the dollar down in 2003.
In both periods, there were significant structural
shifts. The WTO accession in 2002, and the success

### Page 10

10
of Chinese industrial policies in the “green” industrial
sectors and autos in 2022. In both cses exports were
turbocharged when structural shifts that should have
resulted in a stronger currency were instead amplified
by currency weakness.
No matter. Paul Krugman has noted that a country
that is converging technologically with its trade
partners should see a trend appreciation in its
currency. China got the technological convergence,
but not the real appreciation—at least not until this

year. And the move since the middle of 2025 has
corrected the depreciation of the RMB that occurred
when the RMB followed the dollar down on
liberation day, but not the structural overvaluation.
Now, there is an argument that exchange rate
adjustment is too hot an issue to make it a priority.
The PBOC isn’t interested it seems.
And some believe that in the interest of comity and
good relations with China, the IMF should mirror
the PBOC’s views. After all a stronger RMB would

slow China’s exports and thus slow China’s economy
at a time when China needs export growth to escape
deflation and generate employment. Or so the
argument goes. Concerns about “external balance“
and the negative global spillovers from China’s rising
goods surplus should be ignored and the Fund’s sole
focus should be on policies that lead to internal

### Page 11

11
balance. That provides one rationale for the Fund’s
ongoing decision to essentially ignore the contentious
issue of the PBOC’s ongoing exchange rate
management.
That argument, however, assumes that China’s
economy is small and thus China’s external surplus
can safely be ignored because it isn’t a drag on the
growth of the IMF’s other members.
That clearly isn’t true.
And it isn’t clear that China is more interested in
having a negotiation over its level of basic pension

benefits or the size of its fiscal deficit (where the
IMF continues to make timid recommendations that
would not move the needle—and certainly wouldn’t
meaningfully reduce China’s external surplus) than a
discussion over its currency practices.****
Back in the world of fixed but adjustable exchange
rates, there was an expectation (not always met to be
sure) that surplus countries would allow their
currencies to strengthen to bring their economies

back into external balance, and that other policy tools
would be used to ensure internal balance.
I would argue that China’s exchange rate (against
both the dollar and the basket) has many similarities
to the old world of fixed but adjustable currencies.
True, it moves on a controlled path when it moves

### Page 12

12
between different levels rather than jumping up and
down. But the PBOC does frequently target a level,
and that targeted level does change over time.
I would further argue that steps like the recent tariff
rate quota that will limit China’s exports of hybrid
cars to Europe to around 350,000 units next year (up
from 2025, but well below the 800,000 units of
2026) aren’t going to address the underlying
imbalance on their own. They can provide a bit of a

buffer for specific sectors.
But that buffer, while necessary, won’t address the
broad-based erosion of manufacturing capacity
outside China. This erosion will persist as long as an
undervalued RMB sends the entire world a signal to
use China as its production base.
The new system of managed trade that is emerging in
autos would need to be progressively expanded to an
ever-increasing set of sectors if protection is used as a
substitute for both domestic reform in China and

exchange rate adjustment.
That is now the direction of travel, but it doesn’t
address the underlying systemic issues.
Incidentally, those who believe the path of internal
demand is critical to rebalancing and that exchange
rate moves are secondary need to explain how an
acceleration in domestic demand would, on its own,

### Page 13

13
lead China’s external accounts to adjust. One
important, stylized fact about China is that domestic
demand growth is no longer feeding over to import
growth (ex-gold, that is).
Bottom line: China, which needs a 1.5 percentage
point contribution from net exports every year, is on a
collision course with its trading partners without a
currency correction that provides the basis for more
balanced trade even in the absence of trade
restrictions.
And there isn’t a realistic path towards balance that

doesn’t involve a stronger RMB; there aren’t, as Paul
Krugman once put it, immaculate transfers.
* That downturn shows up in domestic deflationary
pressure, and weak job growth/high rates of (at least
youth) unemployment. China, in other words, isn’t in
“internal balance.” I really liked the report from

### Page 14

14
Kamakshya Trivedi and Hui Shan of Goldman Sachs
arguing China needs two tools to hit two targets
(internal and external balance).
** To be sure, rate differentials matter for exporter
conversion—but so does the expected movement of
the RMB. The market understands this better than
the Fund. The Chen and Suwanapruti (Goldman
Sachs) note uses a framework similar to mine to
describe the CNY’s moves in Q3:
“In our view, sizeable FX inflows have continued to support

RMB appreciation despite unfavorable interest rate
differentials, as strong export receipts and increased exporter
FX conversions have outweighed portfolio outflows. Our
estimated FX conversion ratio rose to 63% in August,
compared with around 45% in normal years, likely reflecting a
shift in market expectations from RMB depreciation to
appreciation.”
*** Š ef č ovi č  has jurisdiction over trade policy
(narrowly defined), but not broader macroeconomic

policies. Europe’s institutional architecture for
exchange rate coordination is not well-developed.
**** The current account surplus is no longer masked
to the same extent as in 2023 and 2024, as SAFE has
fiddled with its BoP methodology once again (per the
Federal Reserve) and the BoP goods surplus is no
longer significantly below the customs goods surplus.
Of course, there are still problems in the data—the
deficit in the investment income account most

《歐洲信用債市受法國財政疑慮波及》
歐洲信用市場雖受 AI相關債券波動影響較小 ，但因
能源價格高企 ，加上近期法國主權和財政風險加劇 ，
歐洲信用債市 十月份表現也有不小波動 ，尤其是非
投資級債信用利差一度擴大逾 60個基點。歐洲銀行
債是各產業信用債主權風險曝險較高的類別 ，因歐
洲銀行多數不及美國銀行營運遍及全球且業務多元 ，
此外也持有不少主權債資產 ，十月份市場表現也反
映此一狀況 ，主權債的波動更加重了歐洲銀行債的
跌幅，法國銀行債利差擴大幅度最為明顯 ，所幸法
國主要銀行包括法國巴黎銀行以及興業銀行3Q26獲

利狀況良好 ，即使經營環境惡化 ，從不良貸款率與
信用風險成本等資產品質指標來看法國巴黎銀行持
平前季、法國興業銀行僅微幅上升。
目前市場關注的焦點仍是法國財政整頓的路徑 ，法
國目前的債務負擔約佔 GDP的118%至119%，高盛
預計，如果沒有更有效的財政調整 ，到2030年初負
債比率將進一步上升至接近 125%，如要穩定債務
佔GDP的比重，法國需要實現約佔 GDP 1%的財政
盈餘，但目前的財政赤字約佔 2.5% GDP。法國政
府當局可能在年底前通過一項撙節的預算案 ，若沒
通過法國主權債與信用債可能進一步惡化 ，但即使
通過法國主權債利率要大幅回落的機會仍不大 ，可
能要到2027年法國大選後主權債才有機會趨於穩定 ，
屆時法國銀行債或有布局機會。

重點摘要
• 高利率持續下信用基本面都
已 出 現分 化，如CCC等級的
償債能力指標顯著疲弱
• 衡量整體債信風險仍是歐洲
較美國為高
《非投資級債美國仍優於歐洲》
儘管目前不論是美國還是歐洲的非投資級債在高利
率持續下信用基本面都已出現分化 ，如CCC等級的
償債能力 指標顯著疲弱 ，但衡量整體債信風險仍是
歐洲較美國為高；原因包括歐洲的債務到期牆較美
國明顯，且美國非投資級債2028年前到期的債券分
佈在相對廣泛的行業 ，而當前的賤售債券主要集中
在媒體、原物料與電信 、非必需消費等特定領域 ，
上述較少重疊降低落入賤售債券利差區間的發行人
同時面臨債務到期後償債或再融資令違約風險增加
的可能。另一方面 ，歐洲非投資級債的到期牆更為

陡峭，且市場利率與2028年前到期債券的票面利率
差距大於美非投資級債 ，反映再融資壓力更鉅 ，尤
其是歐洲CCC等級。
分析歐洲非投資級債各產業債務到期狀況可發現
2028年前到期債務產業相對集中 ，且易受景氣影響
的非必需消費在到期債務與賤售債券都佔有一定比
例，另外槓桿率本就較高的電信業佔 2028年前到期
債務9%，融資成本大幅上漲恐令這些發行人債信體
質進一步惡化。

重點摘要
1
分析師／林亞瑱
研究範疇／歐洲總經政策與市場分析
《財政與政治不確定性壓抑估值，歐股延續震盪》
市場對法國財政可持續性以及其可能產生的外溢擔
憂升溫，牽動十月以來歐股走勢震盪，銀行股跌勢
較重，所幸食品飲料與健護等防禦型產業 、能源類
股上揚支撐歐股收紅。評估目前情勢屬法國財政信
心危機，而非歐洲主權風險全面升溫；融資成本上
升對歐洲企業帶來壓力，然整體槓桿可控，財務體
質脆弱的企業面臨較大風險。隨著預算協商進行、
財政擔憂短線難消除，歐央12月利率會議可能再升
息一碼，公債殖利率預估將維持高位，加上中東地
緣風險未除，將對市場情緒與歐股評價帶來壓力 ，
預估歐股短線將延續震盪走勢，維持中立評等。

• 預估現任總理勒克努仍有機

資料來源：野村，2026/10/7，中國信託商業銀行彙整，2026/10。
2027年預算提案樽節開支，但將面對政治角力
《法國財政疑慮、政治僵局與社會動盪導致法德利
差擴大，明年大選前政治風險溢酬預估維持高檔》
十月份法國公債殖利率大幅上升 ，反映法國政治
局勢分裂 、財政整頓前景不明 ，加上明年總統大
選恐由極右派勝出 ，市場對法國財政可持續性的
疑慮升高 ，法國十年期公債殖利率 10月下旬一度
突破5%，創2002年7月以來新高。

近期法國各地爆發大規模抗議活動 ，亦對市場帶
來衝擊 。法國政治與財政風險升溫 ，反而吸引資
金轉向德國公債避險 ，加上近期金融條件趨緊 、
市場對歐央升息的預期相較九月底降溫 ，推動德
國十年期公債殖利率自高檔回落 ，法德利差大幅
擴大 、10/22一度突破 170個基點 ，創2011年11
月歐債危機以來新高(統計至10/26)。
針對2027年預算 ，法國政府提出 430億歐元財政
整頓方案，目標將財政赤字占 GDP比率自2026年
的5.4%降至2027年的5%，措施包括凍結公部門
薪資、限制每月超過1,260歐元的退休金通膨調整
幅度、延長針對大型企業的特別稅但縮小適用範
圍等。反對黨已對多項措施表達反對 ，若不採取
任何樽節措施 ，2027年法國財政赤字占 GDP比率
預估將達6.5%。由於大選在即 ，從近期極右派國
民聯盟釋出的意向來看 ，雖不支持預算案 ，卻也
不願在大選前引發政治危機 ，預估現任總理勒克
努仍有機會透過援引 《憲法》第49.3條於年底前
通過一份財政整頓力度較有限的預算案 。然而，

即使預算案過關，也難扭轉法國債務攀升趨勢。

重點摘要
3
近來融資成本 上升進一步加重法國的債務負擔 。
信評機構 惠譽(2026/10/12)上調利息支出預測 ，
預估2026年至2028年間，利息支出將累積佔GDP
的0.8%。利息支出占GDP比率預估將由2026年約
2.5%升至 20 28年約3.3%，2030年進一步升至
3.8%，反映高融資成本對財政的壓力將逐步顯現 。
利息支出增加將壓縮其他公共支出及財政空間 。
若赤字改善幅度不如預期 ，債務比率恐持續上升 。
2027年總統大選亦將牽動法國財政政策與債市走
向。法國總統大選預計於 2027年4至5月舉行，第
一輪投票訂於 4/18，若無候選人取得過半選票將
於5/2進行第二輪投票。最新民調顯示，極右派國
民聯盟候選人勒龐在首輪支持度領先 ，超過三成；

極左派法國不屈黨候選人梅朗雄近期支持度上升 ，
引發市場擔憂將發生沒有中間派候選人進入第二
輪決選的不利情境。
梅朗雄主張擴張性財政政策 ，對市場最為不利 ，
勝選機率較高的勒龐 10/6提出財政整頓方案並且
主張建立一個可信的債務穩定框架 ，試圖安撫市
場對法國財政前景的疑慮 。在面對市場壓力下 ，
勒龐表達更具財政紀律的立場有助稍稍緩和財政
擴張的擔憂 ，但其財政整頓方案的政策假設與具
體實施存在不確定性 ，加上大選後很可能緊接著
進行國會改選 ，政治版圖維持分裂狀態不利財政
改革。
資料來源：Fitch，2026/10/12，中國信託商業銀行彙整，2026/10。

重點摘要
4
整體而言 ，財政和政治不確定性預估將令法德利
差維持在高位 ，下檔情境為若 2027年預算案無法
於年底前通過 ，恐將面臨更大的財政惡化與市場
壓力，十年期法德利差恐有進一步擴大的風險。
《目前情勢屬法國財政信心危機，而非歐洲主權風
險全面升溫》
儘管投資人對法國債券要求更高的風險補償 ，但
近期法國公債標售仍有足夠的認購需求 。除此之
外，目前法德利差擴大對整體歐洲市場的外溢影
響尚屬可控 ，多數歐洲國家相對德國利差雖受牽
動近期亦出現較明顯的擴大 ，但利差水準仍可控 。
以政府債務占 GDP比重較高的義大利來看 ，十年
期義德利差 10/22一度升至 124個基點 ，回升至
2025年4月水準，但仍遠低於 2011年歐債危機時

期。因此，目前情勢仍偏向法國自身的財政信心
危機，而非歐洲主權信用風險全面升溫。
資料來源：Bloomberg，2024年來至2026/10/21，中國信託商業銀行彙整，2026/10。

《歐元區PMI維持擴張但成長放緩，通膨壓力面臨
上行風險》
歐元區10月綜合PMI初值由9月的53.1降至51.9，
雖連續四個月高於 50、顯示經濟持續擴張 ，但成
長動能有所放緩 。法國受到大規模罷工與抗議活
動影響 ，商業活動下滑 ，成為主要拖累因素之一 ，
法國10月綜合PMI初值降至48.4，明顯落後其他
歐元區國家 。另一方面 ，能源及投入成本 上升迫
使企業提高售價，通膨壓力未明顯緩解。
中東緊張局勢延宕，布蘭特原油期貨價格升至116

美元/桶，TTF天然氣期貨價格升至96歐元/兆瓦時，
天然氣價格持續突破今年高點(統計至10/26)。
目前能源價格高於歐洲央行九月的基準與不利情
境預測 ，歐元區通膨面臨上行風險 ，不過未出現
明顯的二輪通膨效應 ，經濟成長則面臨下行壓力 ，
預期歐洲央行連續升息機率低 。此外，近期公債
殖利率上升亦發揮了緊縮效果 ，預估歐洲央行將
暫時按兵不動 。待12月份利率會議 ，屆時歐洲央
行將取得更新的經濟預測 ，可據此重新評估通膨
與成長前景 ，若能源價格及通膨壓力持續偏高 ，
不排除12月將再升息一碼 ，將存款機制利率調升
至2.75%。
針對近期主權債券殖利率大幅走升 ，預估歐洲央
行將密切關注其走勢 ，但長債利率的上升尚未達
到表明主權債券市場面臨顯著壓力的水平 ，應不
足以觸發政策調整。
資料來源：摩根士丹利，2026/10/22，中國信託商業銀行彙整，2026/10。

重點摘要
7
《融資成本上升對歐洲企業帶來壓力，然整體槓桿
率尚可控，財務體質脆弱的企業面臨較大風險》
主權債券市場的壓力令歐洲企業信用利差同步擴大，
自9月以來，歐洲投資等級債信用利差擴大31個基
點至108個基點，非投資等級債信用利差擴大幅度
較為顯著，擴大105個基點至363個基點，兩者均
回升至2025年5月以來高點(統計至10/26止)。所幸，
歐洲企業資產負債表整體仍維持穩健。儘管淨債務
/EBITDA比率自低檔回升，但整體槓桿水準與歷史
水準相比並不高，仍處健康水準。因此，債務成本
上升雖增加企業的財務負擔，尤其是財務體質較脆
弱的企業面臨較高的風險，但目前尚不至於構成系
統性威脅。另一方面，融資成本上升，企業獲利可

能面臨壓力 ，根據高盛證券 (10/23)預估，在其他
條件不變下，債務成本每上升100個基點，道瓊歐
洲600企業每股盈餘 (EPS)將減少約3%，其中財務
體質較脆弱的企業所受衝擊最大，具備強健資產負
債表與穩定利潤率的企業較能抵禦高利率影響。
《利率維持高位壓抑估值，歐股短線將延續震盪》
隨著預算協商進行、財政擔憂短線難消除，歐央12
月利率會議可能再升息一碼，公債殖利率預估將維
持高位，加上中東地緣風險未除，近期市場對美國
期中選舉後，美伊敵對行動可能重燃的預期升溫，
均將對市場情緒與歐股評價帶來壓力，預估歐股短
線將延續震盪走勢，所幸，LSEG I/B/E/S(10/22)統
計，道瓊歐洲600企業3Q26財報公布雖仍在初期階
段，但目前整體獲利優於財報季前預測，2026年企
業獲利預估相較10月初呈現上修趨勢，歐洲企業獲
利成長應有助提供下檔支撐，歐股維持中立評等。
資料來源：高盛證券，2026/10/23，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10。

### Page 1

微软等美国企业向特朗普政府游说，反对光模块禁令，警告此举可能令数据中⼼建设 “ 陷⼊
停滞 ” 。消息刺激 A 股 CPO 强势反弹，剑桥科技等多股涨超 10% 。与此同时，新易盛披露扩
产进展并预计⽑利率保持稳定， Lumentum 单季营收同⽐增⻓ 136.5% 并斩获超 50 亿美元
CPO 采购承诺，产业链需求信号持续密集落地。
摘要：
11 ⽉ 11 ⽇， A 股 CPO （共封装光学）板块迎来⼀轮集体反弹，超 116 亿元主⼒资⾦单⽇涌⼊。
截⾄午间收盘，剑桥科技、⻘⼭纸业、天洋新材涨停；太⾠光涨超 12% ，新易盛涨超 9% ，中际
旭创、源杰科技涨超 8% 。下午⾏情进⼀步扩⼤，中际旭创、新易盛、衡东光、太⾠光、源杰科
技等多股涨幅超过 10% ，创业板指涨幅扩⼤⾄ 2% 。
驱动这波⾏情的，是来⾃⼤洋彼岸的⼀则消息 ——微软正在游说特朗普政府，要求叫停光模块
的封禁计划。据观察者⽹援引媒体报道，微软及其他美国企业和⾏业组织⼀直在向特朗普政府
游说，警告禁令 “ 可能进⼀步拖慢数据中⼼建设 ” 。

微软出⼿游说，封禁计划遭遇阻⼒
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

据观察者⽹，当地时间 11 ⽉ 10 ⽇，三名知情⼈⼠透露，今年 8 ⽉媒体⾸次披露美国政府将限制相
关国家光模块后，微软等⼏家企业曾与⽩宫科技政策办公室和国家安全委员会官员会⾯，专⻔
反映实施禁令可能带来的供应链问题。
美国信息技术产业理事会（ ITI ）政策与法律事务副总裁直⾔，对光模块的禁令 “ 可能真的会让数
据中⼼建设陷⼊停滞 ” 。他解释称，许多美国云服务企业已经采购了⽤于数据中⼼扩建的 AI 芯
⽚， “ 但如果没有光模块，这些芯⽚就只能闲置，直到企业获得⾜够的光模块，才能将相关设备
连接起来 ” 。他还指出，部分⼤型数据中⼼需要数百万个光模块才能完成设备连接。
市场情报公司 Cleanview 数据显⽰，⽬前微软在美国有 35 个数据中⼼项⽬正在建设，另有 134 个
项⽬处于规划阶段。这⼀体量，使其对光模块供应链的依赖尤为突出。
禁令逻辑存疑，替代⽅案短期难以落地
反对声⾳不仅来⾃企业层⾯，技术层⾯的质疑同样存在。⼀份针对美国联邦通信委员会（ FCC ）
拟议规则的分析指出，光模块中的微控制器⽆法获取⽐特层⾯以上的信息，也⽆法主动向外传

输数据，相关安全担忧在硬件技术逻辑上存在偏差。
替代⽅案的可⾏性同样受到质疑。美国企业 Coherent 和 Lumentum 虽然具备⽣产具有竞争⼒的
光模块产品的能⼒，但美国创新基⾦会报告指出，这两家企业的产能规模不⾜以取代相关供应
商。两家⼤型⽹络设备供应商也向美国科技⾏业媒体 Fierce 表⽰，将光模块⽣产转移⾄美国本⼟
将⾯临⽣产成本、供应链和劳动⼒等多重障碍，建⽴美国本⼟⽣产体系可能需要数年时间。
据摩根⼠丹利 10 ⽉的⼀份客⼾报告，特朗普政府正在考虑将禁令⻔槛设定为每秒 3.2 太⽐特数据
的光模块。这类产品预计要到 2028 年才会开始扩⼤产量，或许能于 2029 年实现⼤规模部署。
⻰头扩产提速，需求端信号密集落地
政策层⾯的不确定性并未阻挡产业链的扩张步伐。
CPO ⻰头剑桥科技在前⼀交易⽇的投资者关系活动记录中披露，截⾄ 2026 年 9 ⽉初，公司已基
本形成⾼速光模块⽉设计产能约 100 万⽀；拟于 2027 年底前，在现有产能基础上将⾼速光模块年
产能再扩充⾄少 1000 万⽀，新增产能分配⾄中国嘉善、⻢来西亚、墨西哥及美国。公司表⽰，

⽬前在⼿订单充⾜，已对多项关键物料提前下单锁定产能，为 2027 年的⼤批量交付做好准备。
光模块⻰头新易盛在互动平台表⽰， “ 结合未来的⾏业指引及订单预期，公司已进⾏了充分的原
材料及产能准备，以满⾜⽇益增⻓的订单交付需求，公司预计未来⽑利率⽔平可保持相对稳定。 ”
新易盛同时透露，成都与泰国⼯⼚均在⼤规模扩产，⽬前客⼾需求旺盛，订单交付正常。
海外产能扩张信号同样强烈。 10 ⽉下旬，英伟达与矽品精密（ SPIL ）确认扩⼤光引擎制造产
能，以⽀持 CPO 技术及⼤规模计算基础设施的连接需求。双⽅⽬标是在 2027 年年中将相关产能
提升⾄⽬前的 15 倍，并计划到 2028 年底再翻⼀倍。
Lumentum 业绩超预期， CPO 订单突破 50 亿美元
需求端的验证在近期集中落地。光学与光⼦技术供应商 Lumentum 公布 2027 财年第⼀季度业
绩，营收同⽐增⻓ 136.5% ⾄ 12.63 亿美元，调整后每股收益 4.57 美元，均⾼于市场预期。受 AI 数
据中⼼光互联需求推动，公司当季斩获第⼆个重⼤ NPO/CPO 项⽬，获得超过 50 亿美元的采购承

诺。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

2 条评论
Lumentum 总裁兼⾸席执⾏官 Michael Hurlston 表⽰， “ 光学电路交换（ OCS ）需求能⻅度近⼏个
⽉显著提升，有望发展为年营收规模达数⼗亿美元的业务。 ” 公司预计第⼆财季营收为 15.75 亿⾄
16.25 亿美元，远⾼于市场预期的 14.8 亿美元，调整后每股收益指引为 5.90 ⾄ 6.20 美元，也⼤幅
⾼于市场预期的 5.07 美元。
机构层⾯，中信建投证券研报称，光互联在整体投资中的占⽐预计将持续提升， 2027 年 800G 、
1.6T 光模块需求持续⾼增， Scale-up 对应的光互联需求在 2027 年开始成为重要增量， NPO 、
CPO 、 XPO 等新品将迎来规模上量。开源证券通信⾏业⾸席分析师蒋颖表⽰， 2027 年 800G 和

1.6T 光模块需求有望延续⾼速增⻓，国产超节点有望实现规模放量。
国⾦证券认为 CPO 有望迎来加速渗透，核⼼供应链公司持续受益；中信建投指出 FAU 器件将同
时受益传统光模块扩容与 CPO/NPO 架构的量价⻬升红利。
开源证券研报指出， 2027 年 800G&1.6T 光模块需求或持续⾼速增⻓， 2.4T/NPO/XPO 光模块需
求初现， CPO/NPO/OCS 交换机出货量或持续提升，光芯⽚向⾼功率快速升级，磷化钢、薄膜
铌酸锂等新材料需求或快速提升；随着全球 AIDC 建设的推进，普通光纤光缆、保偏光纤、空芯
光纤需求或持续增⻓，光纤光缆景⽓度或稳中有升。在国家政策和国内巨头的共同推动下，
2027 年国产 AI 链有望蓬勃发展， 2027 年或成为国产超节点规模放量之年，建议重视交换⽹络、
计算、 AIDC 等三⼤核⼼⽅向。

### Page 1

十一月 06, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新  IC設計 ‧ 台灣

聯詠 
(3034.TW /3034 TT)

看不到隧道盡頭的光  
重要訊息 
聯詠召開 3Q26法說會。 
評論及分析  
3Q26 財報符合預期。3Q26 營收 310 億元，季增 8.1%、年增 26.0%，主因
智慧型手機季節性備貨，帶動 SMDDIC 出貨增加。毛利率為 40.9%，季減
1.5 個百分點、年增 4.6 個百分點，季減主要受 SoC 比重下降，以及成本上
升與 ASP 調整之間存在時間落差所影響。加計營業費用 55.8 億元，使 3Q26 
EPS 達 9.99 元，季減 3.9%、年增 66.3%，符合凱基預估。 
4Q26 毛利率面臨更多逆風 。管理階層預期 4Q26 營收達 280-292 億元區
間、毛利率達 37-39%，以中位數計算分別季減 7.7%與 2.9 個百分點，三
大產品線營收均呈季減，主因 ：(1)平板庫存調整，以及非中國手機客戶庫存
去化，部分被中國手機客戶 OLED DDIC 需求增加所抵銷，使 SMDDI 產品線
相對具韌性；( 2)大尺寸面板 SoC 出貨季減，非面板 SoC 出貨持平；及 (3)大
尺寸顯示器需求季減，尤其 筆電受 1H26 提前拉貨及漲價壓抑終端需求，使
LDDIC 產品線表現最為疲弱。我們僅微幅上修 4Q26 營收預估至公司財測低
緣，反映價格調整，但我們對消費性電子需求的看法仍 較為保守。我們 下修
4Q26 毛利率預估至 38.6%，季減 2.3 個百分點，主因：( 1)低成本庫存已消
耗完畢；( 2)大尺寸面板 SoC 出貨量下滑；及(3) KGD 成本大幅上升，稀釋
SoC 毛利率。  
我們仍保守看待 2027 年的需求。儘管有新產品支撐，包括車用 eDP TDDI 、
TED、折疊手機 OLED TDDI 、平板及筆電的整合式 解決方案，以及獨立式顯
示 IC 和 tTED 類產品，我們對 消費性 IC 設計產業仍保守看待。我們認為新
產品貢獻難以完全抵銷多重逆風，包括：( 1) AI 帶動的通膨壓抑消費性電子
需求；( 2)記憶體價格 維持高檔，推升終端產品售價，恐衝擊智慧型手機、
PC 及筆電出貨；( 3)晶圓代工與封測漲價持續，且公司表示供需情況仍具挑
戰；及(4)美國期中選舉結果可能大幅改變市場展望。因此，我們調整 2027
年營收預估至 1,103 億元，年減 1.2%，毛利率下修至 39.1%，年減 1.2 個
百分點。  
投資建議 
考量： (1)保守看待終端需求； 及(2)新品貢獻難以抵銷多項營運逆風， 我們
下修 2027 年 EPS 預估至 30.71 元(年減 10.6%)。目標價為 460 元，基於
2027 年 EPS 預估平均之 15 倍不變換算得出。維持「持有」評等。  
投資風險  
總經不確定性； OLED DDI 市場競爭減少。

圖 5：公司概況  圖 6：產品組合 
聯詠成立於 1997 年，於 2002 年上市。公司已成為全球平面
顯示器驅動 IC (DDI) 與系統單晶片 (SoC)解決方案的龍頭供應
廠。受惠於台灣的先進矽晶圓代工服務，聯詠已成功擴大產品
線並獲得國際平面顯示器大廠的重點設計訂單。為了滿足從行
動裝置到大型家用設備的消費電子需求，公司提供全系列產
品，公司表示 2020 年在中國大尺寸面板廠的市占 率為
24.1%。

台灣 聯詠

十一月 06, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 5

ESG圖表定義 
項目 定義 說明 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括
為滿足能源使用而生產的電力(公用事業為出售而生產) 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下

購買之再生能源 
Primary再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者(太陽能、風能、水能、生
物質能、地熱能)之量 
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源

再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例  
CO2約當排放量 
直接 CO2與 CO2約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量(範圍 1排放量) 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳(CO2)、甲烷(CH4)、一氧化二氮(N2O)、氫氟碳化物(HFCS)、全氟化合物
(PFCS)、六氟化硫(SF6)、三氟化氮(NF3)

CO2約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2與 CO2約當排放量(公噸)銷售(百萬元)比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量(範圍 1排放量) 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳(CO2)、甲烷(CH4)、一氧化二氮(N2O)、氫氟碳化物(HFCS)、全氟化合物
(PFCS)、六氟化硫(SF6)、三氟化氮(NF3)

廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄
物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內

廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物

總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮

環保支出 
環保支出總金額 
- 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處
置、處理、衛生、清理支出

員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因(自願或非自願)離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失
能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 = (離開之員工/平均員工人數)*100 
- 平均員工人數 = (本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數)/2 
- 本財年末員工數 = 上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數

女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100

女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100

教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練(如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發展) 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1 天 = 8 小時工作

每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數  
資料來源：Refinitiv，公司資料

### Page 1

十一月 12, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新  筆電代工 ‧ 台灣

緯創 
(3231.TW /3231 TT)

2027年AI營收成長亮眼，獲利率趨勢向上  
重要訊息 
緯創 3Q26 每股盈餘 5.74 元，基本符合預期，毛利率與營利率則優於預
期。4Q26 AI 伺服器與整體營收將呈季增，2027 年展望正向。  
評論及分析  
3Q26 每股盈餘符合預期，獲利率超前。 3Q26 EPS 為  5.74 元，季增  
22%、年增 143%，大致符合凱基預估，但優於市場共識。 3Q26 伺服器營
收占比由 2Q26 的 79% 升至 83%，因出給 Dell ( 美) GB300 之 伺服器出貨
量季增 20% 至近 4,800 櫃，以及緯穎 (6669 TT ，NT$1,680 ，增加持股) 因
新 Trainium3 (T3) 機種放量帶動 ASIC AI 伺服器營收成長。受惠於生產效率
提升與營收增加，3Q26 毛利率穩定，營業利益率較前一季改善。不計緯穎
貢獻，緯創本身 3Q26 營業利益率由 2Q26 的 2.1% 升至 2.3%。3Q26 較
佳的營業利益遭匯兌損失及利息支出抵銷。1Q -3Q26 EPS 為 13.54 元，年
增 111%。 
4Q26- 2027 年營收與獲利率上升趨勢不變。 緯創 GB300/VR200 AI 伺服器 
4Q26 出貨量預計將增至約 6 千櫃，季增近 25% 至全年高峰，因 Dell 以外
有新的客戶貢獻，如聯想 (中) 與 Hewlett Packard Enterprise (HPE ；美)。再
加上緯穎營收成長，凱基預期 4Q26 營收將季增 11%，10 月營收達 4Q26 
營收近 30%。緯創 2026 年 GB300/VR200 出貨量應介於 18-19 千櫃，由 
2025 年的 5.8-5.9 千櫃增加，而今年 VR200 出貨量有限，預計僅約 1 千櫃
上下。2027 年的 VR AI 伺服器機櫃將成為主流，凱基預期緯創總量為可望
達 23-25 千櫃，占全球 VR AI 伺服器供給的 25-30%。Neocloud 及主權需
求將為緯創在 2027-28 年創造出貨上行空間。加上對 CSP 的 VR200 與 
Vera CPU 機櫃出貨 (將於 4Q26 -1Q27 開始量產)，凱基預期緯創 2027 年伺
服器營收將年增 65-70%，伺服器營收占比由  2026 年的  83% 升至  90-
91%。然而，2027 年筆電出貨量將持續年減雙位數百分比 ，營收占比低於  
10%。在客戶結構改善與效率提升下，預期緯創 2027 年營利率將提高。而
為滿足強勁的客戶需求，緯創資本支出預計 將由 2026 年的 500-550 億元增
至 2027 年的近 600 億元。然為反映部分 VR 出貨延遲，凱基下修 2026 年 
EPS 預估至 19.36 元，並預期 2027 年 EPS 為 29.47 元，年增 53%。 
投資建議  
我們維持「增加持股」評等，目標價由 308 元上修至 310 元，相當 2027 年
EPS 的 12 倍。 
投資風險  
需求不如預期、零組件供應限制、 AI 伺服器市場競爭。 
增加持股‧維持 
  收盤價 November 11 (NT$)  174.5

前次目標價  (NT$) 308.0

焦點內容   
1. 3Q26 EPS  5.74 元，大致符合我們預
估，惟獲利率表現優於預期。 
2. 受惠伺服器銷售成長及緯穎  (6669 TT) 
表現強勁，預估 4Q26 GB300及 VR200 
AI 伺服器出貨將持續季增，並帶動
4Q26營益率趨勢向上。 
3. 隨新客戶導入及需求攀升，我們預期產
品組合 優化將帶動 2027 年營益率擴
張。客戶基礎多元化及新專案亦將挹注
未來 2-3年 EPS成長。

圖 6：公司概況  圖 7：伺服器佔整體營收大宗 
從宏碁 (2353 TT，NT$30.4，未評等)分割出來的緯創專營電子硬
體之設計/製造/服務(DMS)業者，產品研發聚焦資通訊(ICT)領域，
筆電、桌電、伺服器、儲存裝置、工業自動化 (IA)、網通與通訊
產品都包含在內。緯創在台筆電代工排名第三大，僅次於廣達
(2382 TT，NT$298，增加持股 )與仁寶(2324 TT，NT$31.15，未
評等)，2025年筆電出貨量為 2,410萬台 (年增 19%)，全球市佔
率為 12-13%。緯創在台灣筆電代工廠同業中的業務分化程度最
高。

### Page 5

台灣 緯創

十一月 11, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 5

ESG 圖表定義 
項目 定義 說明 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括
為滿足能源使用而生產的電力（公用事業為出售而生產） 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下

購買之再生能源 
Primary再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者（太陽能、風能、水能、
生物質能、地熱能）之量  
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源

再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例  
CO2 約當排放量 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化
合物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 (NF3)

CO2 約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量（公噸）銷售（百萬元）比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化
合物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 (NF3)

廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄
物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內

廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物

總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮

環保支出 
環保支出總金額 
- 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處
置、處理、衛生、清理支出

員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因（自願或非自願）離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失
能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 =（離開之員工/平均員工人數）*100 
- 平均員工人數 =（本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數）/2 
- 本財年末員工數 =上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數

女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100

女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100

教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練（如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發
展） 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1 天 =  8 小時工作

每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數  
資料來源：Refinitiv；凱基

### Page 1

十一月 11, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新  IC設計‧美國

Nvidia 
(NVDA.O/ NVDA US)

第三季財報預覽：優異的數字恐難激起投資人熱情  
重要訊息 
輝達預計在 11/17 (美國時間)公布第三季財報並召開法說會。  
評論及分析  
超越財測是市場共識。我們預期輝達 FY3Q27 營收可達 1,150 億美元，略高
於公司財測上緣及市場預期的 1,090 億美元，毛利率受記憶體價格影響 ，微
降至 74%。因設計更改等問題，Rubin 起量從第三季底開始，下游出貨集中
在第四季。放寬 HBM4 的頻寬標準，將加速生產放量，而全年的營收較集中
在第四季。我們預期公司第四季營收指引中位數為 1,250 億美元(+9% 
QoQ)，毛利率受記憶體價格影響下滑到 71%。 
記憶體價格維持高檔，晶片廠商轉嫁勢在必行 。我們預期輝達 2026/2027
年資料中心營收將分別達 3,845 億/6,722 億美元。由於 HBM 價格一年一
調，明年初將一口氣反映落後的漲幅，我們預期 HBM4 明年的報價落在 4-5
美元/Gb，對比今年 HBM3e 約 1.7-2.2 美元/Gb。不考慮庫存狀況和折讓 ，
過去記憶體廠商營收約 占 AI 晶片廠商的 15-25%，目前提升至 50%，明年
上看 70%，大幅壓縮晶片廠商的獲利空間。因此，我們預期記憶體升級降
規以及晶片廠商調漲售價轉嫁 記憶體報價上漲是勢在必行。我們預估輝達明
年將調漲報價 15%。Vera 除了 VR 系列將新增獨立機櫃需求，目前以訂單
來看，可望較今年成長 150-200%，2027 年貢獻高達 600 億美元的營收，
但供應鏈能否跟上是一個關鍵。 目前看法沒有改變。  
市場低估了輝達對於供應鏈的掌握。 展望 2027 年，AI ASIC 出貨將首度超
過 GPU，但是 GPU 仍占產值約七成比重。也許基於成長趨緩、資料中心建
置面臨電力和資本諸多限制以及 AI ASIC 蠶食市占率等憂慮，即使連續 15
季財報數字皆超過市場預估和公司財測，公司的評價始終處於歷史低位。對
於評價來說，是否要視輝達為週期股見仁見智，但是我們認為市場遠遠低估
了輝達對於供應鏈的掌握。充沛的現金流更是強勁的護城河，輝達利用融
資、投資和預付款等各種形式扶植供應鏈的擴產，A I ASIC 就算未來在產值
也超越了 GPU，但是未必能夠有足夠的產能支持。  
投資建議  
維持輝達目標價 345 美元，基於 2027 年(2028 財年) 22 倍的本益比，於歷
史均值，目標本益比已 反映了營收成長減速。 
投資風險  
AI 需求反轉，晶片競爭加劇 。公司管理層變動則為系統性風險。

圖 5：公司概況  圖 6：FY2Q27事業別營收組成  
1993 年成立的 Nvidia 是全球 IC 設計大廠，負責設計並銷售積
體電路 (IC) 產品 (如 CPU、GPU 與 DPU)，主要供 PC、伺服器
與汽車產業應用。公司在分離式桌機 GPU 市場居領先地位，
2020 年市佔率接近 80%。受惠於人工智能浪潮， Nvidia 擴大
其加速運算領域優勢 ，在訓練用 GPU 具有壓倒性的地位，期
望透過人工智慧  (AI) 與高效能運算  (HPC) 推動長期獲利成長。

### Page 5

美國 Nvidia

十一月 11, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 5

項目 定義 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括為滿足能源使用而生產的電力（公用
事業為出售而生產） 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下 
購買之再生能源 
Primary再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者（太陽能、風能、水能、生物質能、地熱能）之量  
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源 
再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例 
CO2 約當排放量 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 
(NF3) 
CO2 約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量（公噸）銷售（百萬元）比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 
(NF3) 
廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內 
廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物 
總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮 
環保支出 環保支出總金額 - 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處置、處理、衛生、清理支出 
員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因（自願或非自願）離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減
或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 =（離開之員工/平均員工人數）*100 
- 平均員工人數 =（本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數）/2 
- 本財年末員工數 =上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數 
女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100 
女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100 
教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練（如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發展） 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1 天 =  8 小時工作 
每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數 
股東治理分數 衡量公司用以反收購工具的有效性 
公司治理分數 衡量公司對最佳治理原則的承諾和有效性 
產品責任分數 衡量公司生產優質產品和服務的能力，且產品是否將客戶的健康、安全、整合性和數據隱私進行綜合考量 
社區關係分數 衡量公司對成為優良公民、保護公眾健康和尊重商業道德的承諾 
勞動力分數 衡量公司在員工工作滿意度、健康、工作場所的安全、多樣性、平等以及員工發展機會方面的成效 
資源使用指標 衡量公司在原物料、能源或水的使用效率，以及是否通過改進供應鏈來尋求更俱生態效率的解決方案

### Page 6

Document 662: 国务院：强化社保基金安全监管和保值增值，扩大企业年金覆盖面

Source PDF: all report pdf/国务院：强化社保基金安全监管和保值增值，扩大企业年金覆盖面.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
李强表⽰，加快补⻬社会保障体系短板，扩⼤企业年⾦覆盖⾯，提⾼个⼈养⽼⾦制度吸引
⼒，⽀持商业健康保险发展壮⼤，稳妥有序推进⻓期护理保险制度建设。增强社保基⾦⻓
期可持续性，完善并落实职⼯养⽼保险全国统筹制度，推进基本医疗保险省级统筹，强化
社保基⾦安全监管和保值增值。
摘要：
11 ⽉ 12 ⽇，国务院以 “ 健全多层次社会保障体系，进⼀步织密扎牢社会保障安全⽹ ” 为主题，进⾏
第⼆⼗⼆次专题学习。国务院总理李强在主持学习时强调，要深⼊学习贯彻总书记关于社会保
障⼯作的重要指⽰精神和党中央决策部署，进⼀步深化社会保障制度改⾰，健全覆盖全⺠、统
筹城乡、公平统⼀、安全规范、可持续的多层次社会保障体系，推动现代化建设成果更多更公
平惠及全体⼈⺠。
中国⼈⺠⼤学教授郑功成作讲解。国务院副总理何⽴峰、刘国中，国务委员谌贻琴作交流发

⾔。
李强在听取讲解和交流发⾔后指出，我国已基本建成世界上规模最⼤、功能完备的社会保障体
系。当前经济社会发展形势呈现许多新特点，⼈⼝结构变化、技术进步、就业⽅式多样化等对
社会保障体系建设提出了新的更⾼要求。要充分认识社会保障在发展全局中的重要作⽤，适应
新形势、解决新问题，推动社会保障事业⾼质量可持续发展。
李强指出，要坚持⽴⾜国情实际，发展中国特⾊社会保障体系。把握好尽⼒⽽为和量⼒⽽⾏的
关系，把社会保障作为惠⺠⽣促发展的重要着⼒点，随着经济发展稳步提⾼社会保障⽔平。把
握好政府主导和社会协同的关系，合理划分单位、个⼈、政府、社会的责任，形成责任共担、
互助共济的⻓效机制。把握好保障基本和多元供给的关系，在保基本、兜底线基础上，努⼒丰
富多元供给，更好满⾜⼈⺠群众多层次多样化需求。
李强强调，要突出重点、加强协同，持续优化完善社会保障制度。加快补⻬社会保障体系短
板，扩⼤企业年⾦覆盖⾯，提⾼个⼈养⽼⾦制度吸引⼒，⽀持商业健康保险发展壮⼤，稳妥有
序推进⻓期护理保险制度建设。完善灵活就业⼈员社会保障制度，逐步提⾼参保率，加强新就

业形态⼈员权益保障。推动社会保障制度更加公平，稳步提⾼城乡居⺠养⽼保障⽔平，发挥好
失业保险功能，健全分层分类的社会救助制度，完善社会福利制度。增强社保基⾦⻓期可持续
性，完善并落实职⼯养⽼保险全国统筹制度，推进基本医疗保险省级统筹，强化社保基⾦安全
监管和保值增值。要适应形势变化，及时修订完善社会保障相关法律法规，进⼀步夯实法治⽀
撑。
来源：新华社

Document 674: 大摩警告：旧存储价格正在见顶，2028年供需或转向宽松

Source PDF: all report pdf/大摩警告：旧存储价格正在见顶，2028年供需或转向宽松.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

摩根⼠丹利指出，美国中选后，预计 2028 年美国数据中⼼约 20% 的新增容量将推迟⾄ 2029
年落地，这可能在 2028 年为供应链制造 " 断层 " ，引发旧存储库存积压⻛险。旧存储受固定
价格⻓期协议约束较少，供需缺⼝收窄将⾯临较⼤的价格波动。上述分析仅针对旧存储，
包括 DDR3/4 、 SLC/MLC NAND 及 NOR 闪存。
摘要：
旧存储芯⽚市场的定价红利正逐步⾛向终结。摩根⼠丹利在最新报告中警告，云端资本⽀出不
确定性上升、消费端需求持续疲软，以及供需关系料将于 2028 年前后趋于平衡，旧存储⾏业的
盈利逻辑已从 " 向上弹性 " 转向 " 向下防御 " 。
据追⻛交易台，摩根⼠丹利发布报告指出，美国中期选举后，预计 2028 年美国数据中⼼约 20%
的新增容量将推迟⾄ 2029 年落地，涉及约 5 吉⽡的建设规模，相当于全球 2028 年新增容量的
10% 。
这⼀延迟可能在 2028 年为科技供应链制造 " 断层 " ，压缩旧存储芯⽚的定价空间，并引发库存积

压⻛险。
与此同时，摩根⼠丹利对 SLC NAND 、 MLC NAND 、 NOR 及 DDR4 四⼤产品的供需模型进⾏更
新后发现，各细分市场均呈现趋于平衡的态势，尤其以 2028 年为甚。
报告对市场的直接影响已经显现：摩根⼠丹利下调了多只旧存储相关个股的评级，并⼤幅削减
⽬标价，同时下调 2027 ⾄ 2028 年盈利预期。分析师将 1.0 倍市净率（ P/B ）作为估值下⾏锚，⽽
⾮以盈利⾼峰作为⽀撑底部，标志着市场对该板块的定价逻辑出现系统性转变。
定价优势成 " 双刃剑 " ，需求⾛弱暴露脆弱性
摩根⼠丹利此前的建仓逻辑建⽴在旧存储芯⽚的差异化定价能⼒上 —— 相较于主流存储，旧存
储受固定价格⻓期协议约束较少，因此在短缺周期中能更快速地提价。然⽽，该⾏现在认为，
这种灵活性正在演变为双向⻛险。
报告指出，云端⽀出可⻅度正在下滑，企业端增量需求的确定性随之减弱；与此同时，消费端
需求⼤幅⾛软，终端客⼾承接进⼀步涨价的能⼒受到明显制约。
摩根⼠丹利的 PC 半导体研究显⽰， 2026 年⼀季度⾄三季度出现了集中采购的 " 拉货 " 现象，但四

季度随即进⼊更深度的库存消化阶段，表明此前的订单规模⾼于实际消费需求，存在透⽀之
嫌。
报告特别说明，上述分析仅针对旧存储领域，包括 DDR3/4 、 SLC/MLC NAND 及 NOR 闪存，不
涉及全球主流 DDR5 或 TLC/QLC NAND 市场。
供需模型更新：短缺窗⼝正在收窄
摩根⼠丹利对 SLC NAND 、 MLC NAND 、 NOR 及 DDR4 的最新供需模型均指向同⼀趋势：供需
缺⼝逐步收窄， 2028 年前后或实现基本平衡乃⾄出现过剩。
具体预测显⽰，2028 年 SLC NAND 、 MLC NAND 和 NOR 的供需缺⼝分别约为 -3% 、 -5%
和 -1% ，⽽ DDR4 则可能出现约 +1% 的供给过剩。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

分析师指出，近期短缺局⾯未必会⽴即消散，但缺⼝收窄本⾝⾜以削弱增量定价能⼒，并早于
盈利⾼点触发股价重估。
基于此，摩根⼠丹利下调了对多个产品线 2027 ⾄ 2028 年的 ASP 假设： NOR 及 DDR4 的 ASP 预测
在 2028 年四季度分别被削减约 23% ⾄ 26% ；部分公司的 SLC NAND ASP 预测降幅则更为显著。
在出货量预测基本不变的情况下，收⼊和⽑利率估算随之下移， 2028 年 EPS 预测普遍被下调
10% ⾄ 30% 以上。
数据中⼼资本⽀出延迟： 2028 年供应链 " ⽓⽳ " ⻛险
美国中期选举后出台的数据中⼼新⽴法是此轮预期转变的重要触发因素。
据摩根⼠丹利美国团队测算，德克萨斯州和俄亥俄州等地的⽴法变化导致约 5 吉⽡的数据中⼼建
设从 2028 年推迟⾄ 2029 年，这意味着美国 2028 年数据中⼼新增容量将同⽐下降约 4% ， 2029 年
则将⼤幅反弹约 38% 。
在全球层⾯，摩根⼠丹利现预计 2028 年全球数据中⼼新增容量同⽐增⻓约 9% ， 2029 年则升⾄约
20% 。
这⼀ " 前低后⾼ " 的节奏对旧存储⾏业构成阶段性压⼒ —— 需求延迟叠加供给持续爬坡，2028 年极
可能形成阶段性供⼤于求的局⾯，届时库存周期⻛险将显著上升。
值得关注的是，摩根⼠丹利维持对全球超⼤规模云⼚商资本⽀出的总量预测不变，预计 2027 年
约 1.6 万亿美元、 2028 年约 1.8 万亿美元，但明确指出若建设进度进⼀步推迟， 2028 年的预测存
在下⾏偏差⻛险。
估值框架转向：以账⾯价值为下⾏锚
⾯对盈利预期的系统性下修，摩根⼠丹利将估值⽅法论调整为以 1.0 倍市净率作为下⾏估值锚，
取代此前以盈利⾼峰作为估值底部的框架。
分析师认为，对于资本密集型 IDM 及晶圆代⼯企业⽽⾔，账⾯价值在周期性低⾕中具有⼼理⽀撑
意义 —— 当盈利暂时承压时，投资者通常会更多地回归净资产基础进⾏估值。
在各细分公司层⾯，⽬标价调整幅度相当显著，但 IDM 类企业与⽆晶圆⼚（ Fabless ）设计公司
之间的处理⽅式有所区别。
报告明确指出， 1.0 倍 P/B 的框架主要适⽤于 IDM/ 晶圆代⼯类企业，对 Fabless 公司并不适⽤同⼀
估值⽅法论，因为后者缺乏资产负债表层⾯的估值地板⽀撑，在周期下⾏中⾯临更⾼的波动⻛
险。
分析师将价格放缓、订单削减及库存消化列为主要下⾏催化剂；云端订单需求重新⾛强，或供
给扩张进度延迟，则是当前情境下最主要的上⾏⻛险。

~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
以上精彩内容来⾃追⻛交易台。

[Table_Summary] 核心观点： 
1) 权益：上周市场放量上涨。①市场方面， 本周权益市场在外部
风险偏好压制与内部政策托底的拉锯， 周线收涨。 稳市场机制
加码（互换便利、回购增持再贷款常态化、类平准基金） ，资
金面出现 ETF 净流入、 成交环比回暖等初步改善迹象， 但杠杆
资金去化未完， 主要是封住下沿、 提供修复弹性。②海外方面，
美国中期选举后的 AI 监管担忧与科技股估值重定价冲击海外
风偏， 且10 月非农数据超预期， 本周即将公布10 月通胀数据
也有超预期风险，12 月加息概率仍强，海外风险偏好仍有压
制。③基本面方面，10 月经济数据预计仍然偏弱，基本面筑
底但政策增量传导仍有时滞， 但盈利底已过， 基本面短期影响
有限。 
2) 固收：上周债市收益率明显下行。①当周催化方面， 华发信用
事件 “预防性赎回” 本周初即完成修复， 周二、 周五股市下跌
带动避险资金回流， 且政策换锚落地资金中枢小幅下移， 资金
面仍有趋势性支撑。②后期来看， 政策利率"换锚"提升短端传
导效率，资金面仍有支撑，基本面仍弱修复，核心支撑未破，
不过当前长端赔率已明显收窄， 且后半周资金价格已经小幅回
升，上周买断式回购虽然缩量续作 2000 亿元，央行连续两次
净回笼中期流动性， 资金面单边宽松的定价可能已经充分， 若
后续若 11/13 的6M 买断式继续缩量续作，11 月短期追多性价
比下降，不过 12 月供给收尾叠加明年地方债预期下降仍有催
化利率下行压缩利差的空间。 
3) 海外：上周美股下行、 美债利率下行、 美元指数上行、 人民币
贬值、黄金上涨、油价下跌。① 美国中选民主党夺下两院，
且横扫州长选举，加大 AI 监管限制担忧，加剧风偏回落。尽
管民主党没有达成 2/3 席位， 无法推翻总统VETO， 难以在联
邦层面通过限制 AI 的法案，但州长选举输掉的州里，包括 
AIDC 规划规模最大的得州、俄亥俄、佐治亚等，得州占美国
2030 年前新增数据中心容量约 1/4，新当选州长 Hinojosa 公
开支持暂停数据中心项目。 ②美国 10 月新增非农就业 16.5
万人，显著高于市场预期的 6.5 万，当前 12 月加息概率预期
回升至 77%，叠加中选的影响，对 AI 资本开支压力以及增长
担忧制约风偏。 
4) 风险提示：海外地缘政治风险超预期、 美联储加息路径超预期。

权益 
上周 市 场 放量 上涨 ， 日 均 成 交 额                                     
18738 亿元， 环比放量 2745 亿元。上证
指数 1.39%，深证成指 3.16%，创业板
指 2.14%，万得全A 3.36% ；沪深 300 
1.73%，小市值指数 4.37%；恒生指数

传媒、电力设备、农林牧渔表现靠前，
通信、煤炭、石油石化表现靠后。风格
上，小盘好于大盘，成长好于价值。 
1）市场方面，本周权益市场在外部风险偏好压制
与内部政策托底的拉锯，周线收涨。稳市场机制
加码（互换便利、回购增持再贷款常态化、类平
准基金） ，资金面出现 ETF 净流入、成交环比回
暖等初步改善迹象， 但杠杆资金去化未完，主要
是封住下沿、 提供修复弹性。2）海外方面，美国
中期选举后的 AI 监管担忧与科技股估值重定价
冲击海外风偏，且 10 月非农数据超预期，本周
即将公布 10 月通胀数据也有超预期风险，12 月
加息概率仍强，海外风险偏好仍有压制。3）基本
面方面，10 月经济数据预计仍然偏弱，基本面筑
底但政策增量传导仍有时滞，但盈利底已过， 基
本面短期影响有限。 
固收 
上周债市收益率整体明显下行。 全周10
年期国债期货上涨 0.42%，30 年期国债
期货上涨 1.23%，5 年期国债期货上涨
0.23%。现券来看，10 年国债活跃券下
行 4.90BP ，30 年 国 债 活 跃 券 下 行
4.40BP，10 年国开下行 4.05BP，5 年国
开下行 3.25BP，3 年国开下行4.75BP，
1 年国开下行3.50BP。 
上周央行7 天逆回购净回笼7340 亿元，
隔夜逆回购净回笼2480 亿元， 买断式逆
回购净回笼 2000 亿元，叠加2000 亿元
国库定存净投放及 1000 亿元 SFISF 投
放，整体净回笼 15230 亿元。本周将有
2260 亿元隔夜逆回购到期。 
1）当周催化方面，华发信用事件“预防性赎回”
本周初即完成修复，周二、周五股市下跌带动避
险资金回流， 且政策换锚落地资金中枢小幅下
移，资金面仍有趋势性支撑。2）后期来看，政策
利率"换锚"提升短端传导效率，资金面仍有支撑，
基本面仍弱修复， 核心支撑未破，不过当前长端
赔率已明显收窄，且后半周资金价格已经小幅回
升，上周买断式回购虽然缩量续作 2000 亿元，
央行连续两次净回笼中期流动性，资金面单边宽

降，不过 12 月供给收尾叠加明年地方债预期下

三、风险提示 
①海外地缘政治风险超预期。若美伊局势持续升级导致油价持续高企，通胀
预期将导致全球长债利率持续高位，压制市场风偏。

②美联储加息路径超预期。若油价持续攀升，市场对加息预期走高推动美联
储加息路径超预期，科技资本融资将受到更加明显压制，市场中期风偏将受
到更为明显的压制。

分析师声明 
本报告署名分析师郑重声明：本人以勤勉的职业态度，独立、客观地出具本报告，保证报告所采用的数据和信息均来
自公开合规渠道，报告的分析逻辑基于本人职业理解，报告清晰准确地反映了本人的研究观点，结论不受任何第三方
的授意或影响。本人薪酬的任何部分不曾有，不与，也将不会与本报告中的具体推荐意见或观点直接或间接相关。

证券研究报告对研究对象的评价是本人通过财务分析预测、数量化方法、行业比较分析、估值分析等方式所得出的结
论，但使用以上信息和分析方法存在局限性。特此声明。

投资评级标准

股票投资评级说明： 行业投资评级说明： 
报告发布日后的 12 个月内，公司股价涨跌幅相对于同期
沪深300 指数的涨跌幅为基准，投资建议的评级标准为： 
报告发布日后的 12 个月内，行业指数的涨跌幅相对于同
期沪深300 指数的涨跌幅为基准， 投资建议的评级标准为： 
买    入： 相对沪深300 指数涨幅 20%以上； 
强于大市： 相对沪深300 指数涨幅 10%以上； 
中    性： 相对沪深300 指数涨幅介于-10%～10%之间； 
弱于大市： 相对沪深300 指数跌幅 10%以上。 
增    持： 相对沪深300 指数涨幅介于 10%～20%之间； 
中    性： 相对沪深300 指数涨幅介于-10%～10%之间； 
卖    出： 相对沪深300 指数跌幅 10%以上。

王翊安 
wade.wang@ctbcsis.com

PCB鑽針 
尖點 (8021 TT)

維持評等  
買進(Buy) 
鑽針規格升級趨勢不變  
⚫ 3Q26營收優於預期，鍍膜鑽針 ASP持續走揚  
⚫ AI平台層數與板厚提升，推升鑽針長徑比  
⚫ 鑽孔精度要求提高，鍍膜針滲透率持續擴大  
鍍膜針 ASP持續走揚：3Q26營收 23.31億元(QoQ+27.6%)，優於我們
及市場預期；毛利率 43.0%(QoQ+1.2ppts) ，EPS 2.92 元，略低於我們
預估。我們認為主要因為鍍膜針銷量比重較上季持平，維持 56%；但
鍍膜鑽針 ASP季增雙位數百分比，相對白針的價格倍數推估達 2.2~2.3
倍，符合我們預期。展望 4Q26，我們預估營收 28.97億元(QoQ+24.3%)，
毛利率 44.7%，EPS 3.68 元，較前次預估下修 ，主要考量尖點採日系鎢
鋼材料，報價對應歐洲 APT價格，而歐洲鎢鋼價格持續居高不下， 原
料成本壓力仍在 ，為目前尖點毛利率主要負面因素 。 
板厚提升推升鑽針長徑比： 展望 2027年，公司在法說會上對鑽針毛利
率給予持平指引，主因鎢原料成本上升及新產能爬坡帶來的毛利率壓
力。然而我們認為，隨 NVIDIA Rubin 放量，其主板層數達 26~32層、
板厚 6mm以上，所需最長鑽針長徑比達 40倍以上；AWS Trainium3、
Google TPU v8 等主流 ASIC專案的 PCB層數亦達 26層，甚至 30層
以上，FC-BGA載板所需長徑比同樣在 20倍以上，將帶動鍍膜針報價
相對 2026有明顯跳升， 我們預估 2027年鍍膜鑽針相對白針的平均價
格倍數將上看 3倍，帶動整體鑽針 ASP提升；加上鑽孔服務產能利用
率回升，帶動毛利率改善，我們預估 2027年毛利率可達 48.5%。 
材料升級加速耗針，鍍膜鑽針比重可望提升： 隨板材規格往 M7 以上
升級，M9以上開始導入 Q布甚至 PTFE，鑽針壽命大幅縮短，加上 PCB
孔數增加，帶動耗針量倍數成長。根據供應鏈調查，目前主流 GPU/ASIC
專案的通孔段採用一套 3~5根鑽針，預鑽針仍採用白針；隨客戶對鑽
孔精度要求提高，未來可望出現預鑽針即採用鍍膜針的設計，推動鍍
膜鑽針出貨比重持續提升。我們預估 2027 年高階鍍膜鑽針佔比上看
70%，高於公司 65%的目標。我們預估 2026/2027/2028 年EPS分別為
9.91/28.05/56.49 元；考量近期市場評價修正，本益比由 28倍下修至 22
倍，以 2027年EPS及22倍本益比推估目標價為 620元，建議買進。  
風險因子：高長徑比產品滲透率不如預期、鎢料短缺。

消費者物価指数（全国・2026 年 9 月） 
～財を中心に物価上昇圧力強まる。＋３％台も視野～ 
経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト 新家義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

25 年12 月以来の＋２％台。コアコアの上昇率も明確に拡大 
本日総務省から発表された26年９月の全国消費者物価指数では、生鮮食品を除く総合（ＣＰＩ
コア）が前年比＋2.1％と、8 月の＋1.7％から上昇率が0.4％Pt拡大した。市場予想通りの結果で
ありサプライズはない。＋２％台は 25年 12月以来のこと。また、季節調整済み前月比でも＋
0.5％と、８月の＋0.1％から伸びを大きく高めており、足元では物価上昇ペースの強まりがみら
れる。 
エネルギー以外でも強い動きがみられた。日銀版コア（生鮮食品及びエネルギーを除く総合）
(単位：％）
